========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm fidgeting, exactly. I mean, I sit at my computer all day for work, so I'm pretty used to being still. I try to maintain good posture and everything. 

But... I guess I do feel a bit... wound up? Like, almost all day long. It's actually kind of why I came in to see you. It's just this feeling like my body is constantly bracing for something to happen. Even when I'm just sitting at my desk, or, you know, trying to relax on the couch after dinner. 

I don't want to make it sound worse than it is! I'm still getting everything done. It's just this tight feeling that doesn't really go away. Is that... is that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right.

Like, it seems to take me longer to finish my reports at work lately because I feel like I have to double-check everything more than I used to. Just to be absolutely sure it's right, you know? Oh, well... I try very hard not to. I really do try to keep my mind productive and on track. But, um... I suppose sometimes my thoughts do get a little bit stuck? It's silly, really, I probably shouldn't even mention it.

And before I even realize it, I’ve spent maybe half an hour just checking my messages or replaying everything that could possibly go wrong. 

I always catch myself eventually and get right back to work, though! I promise I'm not just sitting there wasting time all day. I always get my tasks done. Is that... is that normal to do sometimes? I really hope it doesn't make me sound too scatterbrained.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say they just pop in out of nowhere. I'm usually just... trying to think through how to help her, you know? 

I really try to keep my mind focused on what I'm doing, especially at work. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting there letting my mind wander all over the place when I'm supposed to be working. It's more just that... when I do think about her, which is natural because I care about her so much, it just kind of... spirals a little bit, like I mentioned before. I wouldn't say pain, exactly. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or being dramatic about it! I know there are people dealing with much worse things. \n\nI mean, it's not always the most comfortable feeling, because of course I worry about her. But it's just because I care, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think so? Like I said before, I really try to stay focused on my work. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who just zones out and lets their mind wander when they're supposed to be doing something important. 

If I'm thinking about her, it's usually because I've sat down and decided to check in or, you know, figure out a plan. It doesn't just pop up out of nowhere while I'm typing an email or something. I'm usually very good at keeping things separate! 

I really hope I'm answering this right... I know you've asked about this a few different ways, and I'm so sorry if my answers aren't what you're looking for. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information!

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I know I just said they can snowball a little bit sometimes, but I really wouldn't say I can't dismiss them! If I absolutely have to, I can always push those thoughts away and get back to my computer. 

I don't want to give you the impression that my mind is just... out of my control, or that I'm sitting there completely taken over by things I don't want to think about. I'm actually very diligent about snapping out of it when I need to. I always manage to pull myself together! 

I hope that doesn't sound like I'm contradicting myself from a minute ago? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it really is, and I definitely don't want you to think I'm losing my grip on things. Am I making sense?

Oh, um... well, I wouldn't use the word "excessive," really. I think I just care a lot about the people in my life, and I want to make sure everything is taken care of. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around panicking all day! 

But... I suppose my mind does tend to jump around a bit. I guess I just like to be prepared? Sometimes I catch myself trying to solve problems that haven't even happened yet. But I really try to keep it all under wraps so it doesn't bother anyone else. Is it... is it bad that my mind does that? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by trying to stay on top of everything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh, I feel like I'm repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm not being clear... I really just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. 

When I want to stop... well, like I was mentioning earlier about my work, I really do try to just put my foot down with myself. I'll say, "Daniela, that's enough, you need to focus on this report." Because, like I said before, I'll have that one little thought, and then it just turns into five more, and before I even realize what's happening, a whole half hour has gone by with me just checking my messages or imagining everything that could go wrong with my sister or the house. 

I always try my best to pull myself out of it, though! I don't want you to think I just sit there and let it happen without a fight. I really do try. Am I explaining that okay? I hope I'm not being too repetitive or frustrating you.
